As per the 2006–2007 census by [SIDBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SIDBI), there are 1,468,104 [micro, small and medium enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_and_medium_enterprises#India) in Kerala employing 3,031,272 people.[^cite_note-SIDBI_Report_2010-256][^cite_note-257] The [KSIDC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KSIDC) has promoted more than 650 medium and large manufacturing firms in Kerala, creating employment for 72,500 people.[^cite_note-258] A mining sector of 0.3% of GSDP involves extraction of [ilmenite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmenite), [kaolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaolin), [bauxite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauxite), [silica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silica), [quartz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quartz), [rutile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutile), [zircon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zircon), and [sillimanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sillimanite).[^cite_note-GOK_2005c-259] Other major sectors are [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Kerala), medical sector, [educational sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Kerala), banking, [ship building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_shipyard), [oil refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi_Refineries), infrastructure, manufacturing, [home gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential_garden), animal husbandry and [business process outsourcing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_process_outsourcing).

### Agriculture



Tea plantations at [Munnar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munnar), Kerala

A field

A paddy field at [Palakkad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad_district), also known as *The Granary of Kerala*

The major change in agriculture in Kerala occurred in the 1970s when production of rice fell due to increased availability of rice all over India and decreased availability of labour.[^cite_note-Sinha2003-260] Consequently, investment in rice production decreased and a major portion of the land shifted to the cultivation of perennial tree crops and seasonal crops.[^cite_note-Remesh2010-261][^cite_note-Commission2008-262] Profitability of crops fell due to a shortage of farm labour, the high price of land, and the uneconomic size of operational holdings.[^cite_note-google9-263] Only 27.3% of the families in Kerala depend upon agriculture for their livelihood, which is also the least corresponding rate in India.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018406-264]

Kerala produces 97% of the national output of black pepper[^cite_note-Limca_Book_of_Records-265] and accounts for 85% of the [natural rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_rubber) in the country.[^cite_note-google10-266][^cite_note-Economic_Affairs-267] [Coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut), [tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea), [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee), [cashew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cashew), and spices—including cardamom, [vanilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanilla), [cinnamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinnamon), and [nutmeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutmeg) are the main agricultural products.[^cite_note-ChattopadhyayFranke2006-126]<sup>: 74 </sup>[^cite_note-Newton-268][^cite_note-B.L.MarkoseMarkose2007-269][^cite_note-Pradhan2009-270][^cite_note-PradeepkumarPradeep2008-271][^cite_note-OsellaOsella2000-272] Around 80% of [India](14533.md)'s export quality cashew kernels are prepared in [Kollam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam).[^cite_note-273] The key cash crop is coconut and Kerala ranks first in the area of coconut cultivation in India.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018407-274] Around 90% of the total [cardamom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardamom) produced in India is from Kerala.[^cite_note-eco-230] India is the second-largest producer of cardamom in world.[^cite_note-eco-230] About 20% of the total coffee produced in India are from Kerala.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018409-232] The key agricultural staple is rice, with varieties grown in extensive paddy fields.[^cite_note-VarshneyRzóska1976-275] Home gardens made up a significant portion of the agricultural sector.[^cite_note-Dobbie2006-276]

### Fisheries

With 590 kilometres (370 [miles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mile)) of coastal belt,[^cite_note-277] 400,000 hectares of inland water resources[^cite_note-278] and approximately 220,000 active fishermen,[^cite_note-Commission1961-279] Kerala is one of the leading producers of fish in India.[^cite_note-google13-280] According to 2003–04 reports, about 11 lakh(1.1 million) people earn their livelihood from fishing and allied activities such as drying, processing, packaging, exporting and transporting fisheries. The annual yield of the sector was estimated as 6,08,000 tons in 2003–04.[^cite_note-281] This contributes to about 3% of the total economy of the state. In 2006, around 22% of the total Indian marine fishery yield was from Kerala.[^cite_note-Handbook-282] During the southwest monsoon, a suspended mud bank develops along the shore, which in turn leads to calm ocean water, peaking the output of the fishing industry. This phenomenon is locally called *[chakara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chakara)*.[^cite_note-Gulati1984-283][^cite_note-Journal_of_Kerala_Studies-284] The waters provide a large variety of fish: [pelagic species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelagic_fish); 59%, [demersal species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demersal_fish); 23%, [crustaceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean), [molluscs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mollusca) and others for 18%.[^cite_note-Handbook-282] Around 1050,000 (1.050 million) fishermen haul an annual catch of 668,000 tonnes as of a 1999–2000 estimate; 222 fishing villages are strung along the 590-kilometre (370-mile) coast. Another 113 fishing villages dot the hinterland.

## Transportation

### Roads

Main article: [Roads in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_Kerala)

See also: [Kerala State Road Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_State_Road_Transport_Corporation)



Six lane [NH 544](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_544_\(India\)) section from [Thrissur](179902.md) to [Vadakkencherry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadakkencherry)

Kerala has 331,904 kilometres (206,236 mi) of roads, which accounts for 5.6% of India's total.[^cite_note-eco-230][^cite_note-road-285] This translates to about 9.94 kilometres (6.18 mi) of road per thousand people, compared to an average of 4.87 kilometres (3.03 mi) in the country.[^cite_note-eco-230][^cite_note-road-285] Roads in Kerala include 1,812 kilometres (1,126 mi) of national highway; 1.6% of the nation's total, 4,342 kilometres (2,698 mi) of state highway; 2.5% of the nation's total, 27,470 kilometres (17,070 mi) of district roads; 4.7% of the nation's total, 33,201 kilometres (20,630 mi) of urban (municipal) roads; 6.3% of the nation's total, and 158,775 kilometres (98,658 mi) of rural roads; 3.8% of the nation's total.[^cite_note-\:16-17-286] [Kottayam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottayam_district) has the maximum length of roads among the [districts of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Kerala), while [Wayanad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayanad) accounts for minimum.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018422-287] Most of Kerala's west coast is accessible through the [NH 66](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_66_\(India\)) (previously NH 17 and 47); and the eastern side is accessible through state highways.[^cite_note-Kerala_PWD-288] New projects for hill and coastal highways were recently announced under [KIIFB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Infrastructure_Investment_Fund_Board).[^cite_note-289] National Highway 66, with the longest stretch of road (1,622 kilometres (1,008 mi)) connects [Kanyakumari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanyakumari) to [Mumbai](19189.md); it enters Kerala via [Talapady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talapady) in [Kasargod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasargod) and passes through [Kannur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannur), [Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode), [Malappuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malappuram), [Guruvayur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guruvayur), [Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi), [Alappuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alappuzha), [Kollam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam), [Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram) before entering [Tamil Nadu](29918.md).[^cite_note-Kerala_PWD-288] [Palakkad district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad_district) is generally referred to as the Gateway of Kerala, due to the presence of the Palakkad Gap in the Western Ghats, through which the northern ([Malabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malabar_region)) and southern (Travancore) parts of Kerala are connected to the rest of India via road and rail. The state's largest checkpoint, [Walayar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walayar), is on [NH 544](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_544_\(India\)), in the border town between Kerala and Tamil Nadu, through which a large amount of public and commercial transportation reaches the northern and central districts of Kerala.[^cite_note-290]

The [Department of Public Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Public_Works_\(Kerala\)) is responsible for maintaining and expanding the [state highways system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_State_Highways_in_Kerala) and major district roads.[^cite_note-291] The Kerala State Transport Project (KSTP), which includes the [GIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GIS)-based Road Information and Management Project (RIMS), is responsible for maintaining and expanding the state highways in Kerala. It also oversees a few major district roads.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] Traffic in Kerala has been growing at a rate of 10–11% every year, resulting in high traffic and pressure on the roads. Traffic density is nearly four times the national average, reflecting the state's high population. Kerala's annual total of road accidents is among the nation's highest. The accidents are mainly the result of the narrow roads and irresponsible driving.[^cite_note-294] National Highways in Kerala are among the narrowest in the country and will remain so for the foreseeable future, as the state government has received an exemption that allows narrow national highways. In Kerala, highways are 45 metres (148 feet) wide. In other states, national highways are grade separated, 60 metres (200 feet) wide with a minimum of four lanes, as well as 6 or 8-lane access-controlled expressways.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] The National Highways Authority of India (NHAI) has threatened the Kerala state government that it will give higher priority to other states in highway development since political commitment to better highways in Kerala has been lacking.[^cite_note-297] As of 2013, Kerala had the highest road accident rate in the country, with most fatal accidents taking place along the state's national highways.[^cite_note-298]

### Railways

Main article: [List of Railway Stations in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Railway_Stations_in_Kerala)

See also: [Kochi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi_Metro)

[Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Railway_\(India\)) zone of [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways) operates all railway lines in the state connecting most major towns and cities except those in the highland districts of [Idukki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idukki_district) and [Wayanad](407429.md).[^cite_note-299] The railway network in the state is controlled by two out of six divisions of the [Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Railway_zone); [Thiruvananthapuram Railway division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram_Railway_division) headquartered at [Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram) and [Palakkad Railway Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad_Railway_Division) headquartered at [Palakkad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad).[^cite_note-300] [Thiruvananthapuram Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram_Central) (TVC) is the busiest railway station in the state.[^cite_note-301] Kerala's major railway stations are:

- [Thiruvananthapuram Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram_Central) (TVC)

- [Ernakulam Junction (South)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernakulam_Junction_railway_station) (ERS)

- [Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode_railway_station) (CLT)

- [Kollam Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam_Junction_railway_station) (QLN)

- [Thrissur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrissur_railway_station) (TCR)

- [Palakkad Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad_Junction) (PGT)

- [Kannur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannur_railway_station) (CAN)

- [Shoranur Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shornur_Junction) (SRR)

- [Ernakulam Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernakulam_Town) (North) (ERN)

- [Kottayam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottayam_railway_station) (KTYM)

- [Chengannur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengannur_railway_station) (CNGR)

- [Alappuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alappuzha_railway_station) (ALLP)

- [Thiruvananthapuram North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram_North_Railway_station) (TVCN)

- [Kayamkulam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayamkulam) Junction (KYJ)

- [Tirur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirur_railway_station) (TIR)

- [Kasaragod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasaragod_railway_station) (KGQ)

- [Aluva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aluva_railway_station) (AWY)

- [Thalassery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalassery) (TLY)

### Airports

Main article: [List of airports in Kerala state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Kerala_state)



[Cochin International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_International_Airport), the busiest airport in the state.

Kerala has four international airports–[Cochin International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_International_Airport), [Thiruvananthapuram International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram_International_Airport), [Calicut International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calicut_International_Airport), and [Kannur International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannur_International_Airport). [Kollam Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam_Airport), established under the Madras Presidency, but since closed, was the first airport in Kerala.[^cite_note-302] [Kannur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannur) had an airstrip used for commercial aviation as early as 1935 when [Tata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Group) airlines operated weekly flights between [Mumbai](19189.md) and Thiruvananthapuram – stopping at [Goa](40010153.md) and Kannur.[^cite_note-303] Trivandrum International Airport, managed by the [Airport Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_Authority_of_India), is among the oldest existing airports in South India. [Calicut International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calicut_International_Airport), which was opened in 1988, is the second-oldest existing airport in Kerala and the oldest in the [Malabar region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malabar_District).[^cite_note-304] Cochin International Airport is the busiest in the state and the [seventh busiest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_India) in the country. It is also the first airport in the world to be fully powered by [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIAL_Solar_Power_Project)[^cite_note-BBC-305] and won the [Champions of the Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champions_of_the_Earth) award instituted by the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-UN-306] Cochin International Airport is also the first Indian airport to be incorporated as a [public limited company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_limited_company); it was funded by nearly 10,000 [non-resident Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-resident_Indian_and_person_of_Indian_origin) from 30 countries.[^cite_note-307] Other than civilian airports, Kochi has a naval airport named [INS Garuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Garuda). Thiruvananthapuram Airport shares civilian facilities with the Southern Air Command of the [Indian Air Force](403355.md).

### Water transport

Main article: [Ports in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ports_in_Kerala)

See also: [Kochi Water Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi_Water_Metro)



[Cochin Shipyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_Shipyard)

Kerala has [two major ports, four intermediate ports, and 13 minor ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ports_in_Kerala), 4 of which have immigration checkpoint facilities.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] The major port in the state is at [Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_Port), which has an area of 8.27 km<sup>2</sup> (3.19 sq mi).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310] The [Vizhinjam International Seaport Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizhinjam_International_Seaport_Thiruvananthapuram), which is currently classified as a major port, only completed Phase I as others are under construction.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310] Other intermediate ports include [Beypore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beypore), [Kollam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam), and [Azheekal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azhikode_and_Azhikkal).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310] The remaining ports are classified as minor which include [Manjeshwaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manjeshwaram), [Kasaragod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasaragod), [Nileshwaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nileshwaram), [Kannur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannur), [Thalassery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalassery), [Vadakara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadakara), [Ponnani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnani), [Munambam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munambam), Manakodam, [Alappuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alappuzha), [Kayamkulam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayamkulam), [Neendakara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neendakara), and [Valiyathura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valiyathura).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310] The [Kerala Maritime Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Maritime_Institute) is headquartered at Neendakara, which has an additional subcentre at [Kodungallur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodungallur) too.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310] The state has numerous [backwaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_backwaters), which are used for commercial [inland navigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_navigation). Transport services are mainly provided by country craft and passenger vessels. There are 67 navigable rivers in the state while the total length of inland waterways is 1,687 kilometres (1,048 mi).[^cite_note-google14-311] The main constraints to the expansion of inland navigation are; lack of depth in waterways caused by silting, lack of maintenance of navigation systems and bank protection, accelerated growth of the [water hyacinth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_hyacinth), lack of modern inland craft terminals, and lack of a cargo handling system.

Kerala also have India's first water metro, called 'Kochi water metro'.[^cite_note-312] The Kochi water metro project identified 15 routes, connecting 10 islands along a network of routes that span 78 km with a fleet of 78 fast, and fully electrically propelled hybrid ferries.[^cite_note-313] The 616 kilometres (383 mi) long West-Coast Canal is the longest waterway in state connecting [Kasaragod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasaragod) to [Poovar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poovar).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018423-314] It is divided into five sections: 41 kilometres (25 mi) long Kasaragod-[Nileshwaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nileshwaram) reach, 188 kilometres (117 mi) long Nileshwaram-[Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode) reach, 160 kilometres (99 mi) Kozhikode-[Kottapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottappuram,_Thrissur) reach, 168 kilometres (104 mi) long [National Waterway 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Waterway_3) (Kottapuram-[Kollam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam) reach), and 74 kilometres (46 mi) long Kollam-[Vizhinjam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizhinjam) reach.[^cite_note-eco-230] The [Conolly Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conolly_Canal), which is a part of the West-Coast Canal, connects the city of Kozhikode with [Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi) through Ponnani, passing through the districts of [Malappuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malappuram_district) and [Thrissur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrissur_district). It begins at [Vadakara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadakara).[^cite_note-hindu_jan08-315] It was constructed in the year 1848 under the orders of then [District collector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_collector) of [Malabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malabar_District), H. V. Conolly, initially to facilitate movement of goods to [Kallayi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kallayi) Port from the hinterlands of Malabar through [Kuttiady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuttiady) and [Korapuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korapuzha) river systems.[^cite_note-hindu_jan08-315] It was the main waterway for the cargo movement between Kozhikode and Kochi through Ponnani, for more than a century.[^cite_note-hindu_jan08-315] Other important waterways in Kerala include the Alappuzha-[Changanassery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changanassery) Canal, Alappuzha-Kottayam-[Athirampuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athirampuzha) Canal, and Kottayam-[Vaikom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaikom) Canal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018424-310]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Kerala)

See also: [Ethnic groups in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Kerala) and [List of people from Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_from_Kerala)



The [Population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid) of Kerala

Kerala is home to 2.8% of India's population; with a density of 859 persons per km<sup>2</sup>, its land is nearly three times as densely settled as the national average of 370 persons per km<sup>2</sup>.[^cite_note-Census_2011-316] As of 2011, Thiruvananthapuram is the most populous city in Kerala.[^cite_note-Census2011cities-317] In the state, the rate of population growth is India's lowest, and the decadal growth of 4.9% in 2011 is less than one-third of the all-India average of 17.6%.[^cite_note-Census_2011-316] Kerala's population more than doubled between 1951 and 1991 by adding 15.6 million people to reach 29.1 million residents in 1991; the population stood at 33.3 million by 2011.[^cite_note-Census_2011-316] Kerala's coastal regions are the most densely settled with population of 2022 persons per km<sup>2</sup>, 2.5 times the overall population density of the state, 859 persons per km<sup>2</sup>, leaving the eastern hills and mountains comparatively sparsely populated.[^cite_note-kerala_fact_sheet-318] Kerala is the [second-most urbanised major state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanisation_in_India) in the country with [47.7% urban population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Kerala_by_urban_area_growth) according to the [2011 Census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Census_of_India).[^cite_note-ubn-319] Around 31.8 million Keralites are predominantly Malayali.[^cite_note-Census_2011-316] The state's 321,000 indigenous tribal *[Adivasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adivasi)*, 1.1% of the population, are concentrated in the east.[^cite_note-Kalathil_2004-320]<sup>: 10–12 </sup>

### Gender

See also: [Women in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Kerala)

There is a tradition of matrilineal inheritance in Kerala, where the mother is the head of the household.[^cite_note-321] As a result, women in Kerala have had a much higher standing and influence in the society. This was common among certain influential castes and is a factor in the value placed on daughters. Christian missionaries also influenced Malayali women in that they started schools for girls from poor families.[^cite_note-322] Opportunities for women such as education and gainful employment often translate into a lower birth rate,[^cite_note-323] which in turn, make education and employment more likely to be accessible and more beneficial for women. This creates an upward spiral for both the women and children of the community that is passed on to future generations. According to the Human Development Report of 1996, Kerala's [Gender Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_Development_Index) was 597; higher than any other state of India. Factors, such as high rates of female literacy, education, work participation and life expectancy, along with favourable [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio), contributed to it.[^cite_note-Joseph-324]

Kerala's sex ratio of 1.084 (females to males) is higher than that of the rest of India; it is the only state where women outnumber men.[^cite_note-Tharamangalam_2005-228]<sup>: 2 </sup> While having the opportunities that education affords them, such as political participation, keeping up to date with current events, reading religious texts, etc., these tools have still not translated into full, [equal rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_rights) for the women of Kerala. There is a general attitude that women must be restricted for their own benefit. In the state, despite the social progress, gender still influences [social mobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_mobility).[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

#### LGBT rights

Main article: [LGBT rights in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Kerala)

Kerala has been at the forefront of LGBT issues in [India](14533.md).[^cite_note-328] Kerala is one of the first [states in India](375986.md) to form a welfare policy for the transgender community. In 2016, the Kerala government introduced free [sex reassignment surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_reassignment_surgery) through government hospitals.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] [Queerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queerala) is one of the major LGBT organisations in Kerala. It campaigns for increased awareness of LGBT people and sensitisation concerning healthcare services, workplace policies and educational curriculum.[^cite_note-332] Since 2010, [Kerala Queer Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Queer_Pride) has been held annually across various cities in Kerala.[^cite_note-333]

In June 2019, the Kerala government passed a new order that members of the transgender community should not be referred to as the "[third gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_gender)" or "other gender" in government communications. Instead, the term "transgender" should be used. Previously, the gender preferences provided in government forms and documents included male, female, and other/third gender.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335]

### Human Development Index

See also: [Kerala model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_model)



[Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) map for Indian states in 2006, as calculated by the [Government of India](553883.md) and [United Nations Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme)

[^cite_note-336]

Under a democratic communist state government, Kerala has achieved a record of social development much more advanced than the Indian average.[^cite_note-337] As of 2015, Kerala has a [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) (HDI) of 0.770, which is in the "high" category, ranking it first in the country.[^cite_note-snhdi-gdl-338] It was 0.790 in 2007–08[^cite_note-IDHR_2011-339] and it had a consumption-based HDI of 0.920, which is better than that of many developed countries.[^cite_note-IDHR_2011-339] Comparatively higher spending by the government on [primary level education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_education), [health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care) and the elimination of [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_India) from the 19th century onwards has helped the state maintain an exceptionally high HDI;[^cite_note-UNDP2005-340][^cite_note-UNDP20054-341] the report was prepared by the central government's [Institute of Applied Manpower Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Applied_Manpower_Research).[^cite_note-hdi_kerala_chart-342][^cite_note-hdi_kerala-343] However, the Human Development Report 2005, prepared by Centre for Development Studies envisages a virtuous phase of inclusive development for the state since the advancement in human development had already started aiding the economic development of the state.[^cite_note-UNDP2005-340] Kerala is also widely regarded as the [cleanest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_hygiene) and healthiest state in India.[^cite_note-344]

According to the 2011 census, Kerala has the highest [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate) (94%) among Indian states. In 2018, the literacy rate was calculated to be 96.2% in the 2018 literacy survey conducted by the National Statistical Office, India.[^cite_note-Literacy_survey_2018-345] In the [Kottayam district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottayam_district), the literacy rate was 97%.[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-pc-census2011-11][^cite_note-IBNLiteracy2013-347] The [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) in Kerala is 74 years, among the highest in India as of 2011.[^cite_note-348] Kerala's rural poverty rate fell from 59% (1973–1974) to 12% (1999–2010); the overall (urban and rural) rate fell 47% between the 1970s and 2000s against the 29% fall in overall poverty rate in India.[^cite_note-349] By 1999–2000, the rural and urban poverty rates dropped to 10.0% and 9.6%, respectively.[^cite_note-princeton6-242] The 2013 Tendulkar Committee Report on poverty estimated that the percentages of the population living below the poverty line in rural and urban Kerala are 9.1% and 5.0%, respectively.[^cite_note-350] These changes stem largely from efforts begun in the late 19th century by the kingdoms of Cochin and Travancore to boost social welfare.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] This focus was maintained by Kerala's post-independence government.[^cite_note-Brenkert_Malone_2003-164][^cite_note-Varma_2005-353]<sup>: 48 </sup> Kerala is the [least impoverished state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_poverty_rate) in India according to [NITI Aayog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NITI_Aayog)'s Sustainable Development Goals dashboard and [Reserve Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India)'s *Handbook of Statistics on Indian Economy*.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]

Kerala has undergone a "[demographic transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_transition)" characteristic of such [developed nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_nation) as [Canada](5042916.md), [Japan](15573.md), and [Norway](21241.md).[^cite_note-Tharamangalam_2005-228]<sup>: 1 </sup> In 2005, 11.2% of people were over the age of 60.[^cite_note-Varma_2005-353] In 2023, the BBC reported on the problems and benefits which have arisen from migration away from Kerala, focussing on the village of Kumbanad.[^cite_note-356] In 2004, the birthrate was low at 18 per 1,000.[^cite_note-Kutty_2004_6-357] According to the 2011 census, Kerala had a total fertility rate (TFR) of 1.6. All districts except Malappuram district had fertility rates below 2. Fertility rate is highest in [Malappuram district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malappuram_district) (2.2) and lowest in [Pathanamthitta district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathanamthitta_district) (1.3).[^cite_note-358] In 2001, Muslims had the TFR of 2.6 as against 1.5 for Hindus and 1.7 for Christians.[^cite_note-359] The state also is regarded as the "least corrupt Indian state" according to the surveys conducted by CMS Indian Corruption Study (CMS-ICS)[^cite_note-360] Transparency International (2005)[^cite_note-361] and *India Today* (1997).[^cite_note-362] Kerala has the lowest [homicide](123451.md) rate among Indian states, with 1.1 per 100,000 in 2011.[^cite_note-ncrb-363] In respect of female empowerment, some negative factors such as higher [suicide rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_rate), lower share of [earned income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earned_income), [child marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_marriage),[^cite_note-364] complaints of [sexual harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_harassment) and limited freedom are reported.[^cite_note-Joseph-324] The child marriage is lower in Kerala. The Malappuram district has the highest number of child marriages and the number of such cases is increasing in Malappuram. Child marriages are particularly [higher among the Muslim community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_marriage_among_Muslims_in_Kerala).[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366] In 2019, Kerala recorded the highest child sex abuse complaints in India.[^cite_note-367]

In 2015, Kerala had the highest conviction rate of any state, over 77%.[^cite_note-368] Kerala has the lowest proportion of homeless people in rural India, <0.1%,[^cite_note-369] and the state is attempting to reach the goal of becoming the first "Zero Homeless State", in addition to its acclaimed "Zero landless project", with private organisations and the expatriate Malayali community funding projects for building homes for the homeless.[^cite_note-370] The state was also among the lowest in the [India State Hunger Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_State_Hunger_Index) next only to [Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab,_India). In 2015 Kerala became the first "complete digital state" by implementing e-governance initiatives.[^cite_note-371]

### Healthcare



[Regional Cancer Centre, Trivandrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Cancer_Centre,_Trivandrum)

See also: [List of government hospitals in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_government_hospitals_in_Kerala)

Kerala is a pioneer in implementing the [universal health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care) program.[^cite_note-372] The [sub-replacement fertility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility) level and [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate) are lower compared to those of other states, estimated from 12[^cite_note-Brenkert_Malone_2003-164][^cite_note-Kutty_2004_6-357]<sup>: 49 </sup> to 14[^cite_note-Krishnaswami_2004-373]<sup>: 5 </sup> deaths per 1,000 live births; as per the National Family Health Survey 2015–16, it has dropped to 6.[^cite_note-374] According to a study commissioned by [Lien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lien_Ying_Chow) Foundation, a [Singapore](27318.md)-based philanthropic organisation, Kerala is considered to be the best place to die in India based on the state's provision of palliative care for patients with serious illnesses.[^cite_note-375] However, Kerala's [morbidity rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morbidity_rate) is higher than that of any other Indian state—118 (rural) and 88 (urban) per 1,000 people. The corresponding figures for all India were 55 and 54 per 1,000, respectively as of 2005.[^cite_note-Krishnaswami_2004-373]<sup>: 5 </sup> Kerala's 13.3% [prevalence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prevalence) of [low birth weight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_birth_weight) is higher than that of many [first world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_world) nations.[^cite_note-Kutty_2004_6-357] Outbreaks of [water-borne diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water-borne_disease) such as [diarrhoea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diarrhoea), [dysentery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysentery), [hepatitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis), and [typhoid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoid) among the more than 50% of people who rely on 3 million [water wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_well) is an issue worsened by the lack of [sewers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanitary_sewer).[^cite_note-Roy_2004-376]<sup>: 5–7 </sup> As of 2017, the state has the highest number of diabetes patients and also the highest prevalence rate of the disease in India.[^cite_note-377]

The [United Nations Children's Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Children's_Fund) (UNICEF) and the [World Health Organization](33583.md) designated Kerala the world's first "[baby-friendly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_Friendly_Hospital_Initiative) state" because of its effective promotion of breastfeeding over formulas.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Over 95% of Keralite births are hospital-delivered and the state also has the lowest infant mortality rate in the country. The third National Family Health Survey ranks Kerala first in "Institutional Delivery" with 100% of births being in medical facilities.[^cite_note-kerala_fact_sheet-318] [Ayurveda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayurveda),[^cite_note-Unnikrishnan_2004-380]<sup>: 13 </sup> *[siddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddha)*, and endangered and endemic modes of [traditional medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_medicine), including *kalari*, *marmachikitsa* and *vishavaidyam*, are practised. Some occupational communities such as [Kaniyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaniyar) were known as native medicine men in relation to the practice of such streams of medical systems, apart from their traditional vocation.[^cite_note-381] These propagate via *[gurukula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurukula)* discipleship,[^cite_note-Unnikrishnan_2004-380]<sup>: 5–6 </sup> and comprise a fusion of both medicinal and alternative treatments.[^cite_note-Unnikrishnan_2004-380]<sup>: 15 </sup> The *[Arya Vaidya Sala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arya_Vaidya_Sala)* established by [Vaidyaratnam P. S. Warrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaidyaratnam_P._S._Warrier) at [Kottakkal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottakkal) (about 10 km from [Malappuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malappuram)) in 1902, is the largest Ayurvedic medicinal network and health centre in the state.[^cite_note-Warrier-382][^cite_note-Arya_Vaidya_Sala_Kottakkal_–_Part_1-383][^cite_note-mal-384] It is also one of the largest Ayurvedic medicinal brands in the world.[^cite_note-Warrier-382][^cite_note-Arya_Vaidya_Sala_Kottakkal_–_Part_1-383][^cite_note-mal-384]

In 2014, Kerala became the first state in India to offer free cancer treatment to the poor, via a program called Sukrutham.[^cite_note-385] People in Kerala experience elevated incidence of [cancers](105219.md), [liver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liver_disease) and [kidney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidney_disease) diseases.[^cite_note-386] In April 2016, the *Economic Times* reported that 250,000 residents undergo treatment for cancer. It also reported that approximately 150 to 200 liver transplants are conducted in the region's hospitals annually. Approximately 42,000 cancer cases are reported in the region annually. This is believed to be an underestimate as private hospitals may not be reporting their figures. Long waiting lists for kidney donations have stimulated illegal trade in human kidneys, and prompted the establishment of the Kidney Federation of India which aims to support financially disadvantaged patients.[^cite_note-387] As of 2017–18, there are 6,691 modern medicine institutions under the [Department of Health Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Health_and_Family_Welfare_\(Kerala\)), of which the total bed strength is 37,843; 15,780 in rural areas and 22,063 in urban.[^cite_note-dhs-388]

### Language

[Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam) is the official language of Kerala and one of the [Classical languages of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_India).[^cite_note-thehindu20130524-389][^cite_note-390] There is a significant [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language)-speaking population in [Idukki district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idukki_district) and [Palakkad district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palakkad_district), which accounts for 17.48% and 4.8% respectively of the two districts' populations.[^cite_note-lg-391] [Tulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulu_language) and [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada) are spoken mainly in the northern parts of [Kasaragod district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasaragod_district), each of which account for 8.77% and 4.23% of total population in the district, respectively.[^cite_note-lg-391][^cite_note-392]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Kerala)

**Religion in Kerala** (2011)

[^cite_note-census_2016_religion-393]

1. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Kerala) (54.7%)

2. [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Kerala) (26.6%)

3. [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Kerala) (18.4%)

4. Other or none (0.32%)

Kerala is very religiously diverse with [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindus), [Muslims](19541.md) and [Christians](18337522.md) having a significant population throughout the state. Kerala is often regarded as one of the most diverse states in all of India.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) is the most widely professed faith in Kerala, with significant numbers of adherents to [Islam](6037917.md) and [Christianity](5211.md). In comparison with the rest of India, Kerala experiences relatively little [sectarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectarianism).[^cite_note-396] According to [2011 Census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Census_of_India) figures, 54.7% of Kerala's residents are Hindus, 26.6% are Muslims, 18.4% are Christians, and the remaining 0.3% follow another religion or have no religious affiliation.[^cite_note-censusindia2011religion-397] Hindus represent the biggest religious group in all districts except [Malappuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malappuram_district), where they are outnumbered by Muslims.[^cite_note-thehindu1-398] Kerala has the largest population of Christians in India.[^cite_note-399] As of 2016, Hindus, Muslims, Christians and others account for 41.9%, 42.6%, 15.4% and 0.2% of the total childbirths in the state, respectively.[^cite_note-400]

Islam arrived in Kerala, a part of the larger [Indian Ocean](14580.md) rim, via spice and silk traders from the [Middle East](19323.md). Historians do not rule out the possibility of Islam being introduced to Kerala as early as the seventh century CE.[^cite_note-indiatimes3-401][^cite_note-autogenerated20002-402] Notable has been the occurrence of [Cheraman Perumal Tajuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legend_of_Cheraman_Perumals), the mythical Hindu king who moved to [Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia) to meet [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad) and converted to Islam.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] Kerala Muslims are generally referred to as the [Mappilas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malabar_Muslims). Mappilas are but one among the many communities that form the Muslim population of Kerala.[^cite_note-KunhaliV2-406][^cite_note-Divakaruni20112-407] According to the [Legend of Cheraman Perumals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legend_of_Cheraman_Perumals), the first Indian mosque was built in 624 CE at [Kodungallur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodungallur) with the mandate of the last the ruler (the Cheraman Perumal) of [Chera dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chera_dynasty), who converted to Islam during the lifetime of Muhammad (c. 570–632).[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-SimpsonKresse2008-409][^cite_note-Kupferschmidt1987-410][^cite_note-Raṇṭattāṇi2007-411]

Ancient Christian tradition says that Christianity reached the shores of Kerala in 52 CE with the arrival of [Thomas the Apostle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle), one of the [Twelve Apostles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve_Apostles) of [Jesus](1095706.md) Christ.[^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413][^cite_note-Encyclopædia_Britannica-414][^cite_note-Menachery-415] [Saint Thomas Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians) include [Syro-Malabar Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syro-Malabar_Church),[^cite_note-Ponnumuthan1996-416] [Syro-Malankara Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syro-Malankara_Catholic_Church),[^cite_note-Williams1996-417] [Jacobite Syrian Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobite_Syrian_Christian_Church),[^cite_note-AndersonTang2005-418] [Mar Thoma Syrian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mar_Thoma_Syrian_Church),[^cite_note-419] [Malankara Orthodox Syrian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malankara_Orthodox_Syrian_Church),[^cite_note-Russell2010-420] the [Syrian Anglicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Anglicans) of the [CSI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_South_India)[^cite_note-421] and [Pentecostal Saint Thomas Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal_Saint_Thomas_Christians).[^cite_note-422] The origin of the [Latin Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church) Christians in Kerala is the result of the missionary endeavours of the Portuguese [Padroado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padroado) in the 16th century.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-423][^cite_note-RakRaj2008-424][^cite_note-Subramanian2009-425] As a consequence of centuries of mixing with colonial immigrants, beginning with the Portuguese, Dutch, French, British and other Europeans, there is a community of [Anglo-Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Indians) in Kerala of mixed European and Indian parentage or ancestry. Kerala has the highest population of Christians among all the states of India.[^cite_note-426]

[Judaism](15624.md) reached Kerala in the 10th century BCE during the time of [King Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon).[^cite_note-427] They are called [Cochin Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_Jews) or Malabar Jews and are the oldest group of [Jews in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_India).[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429] There was a significant Jewish community which existed in Kerala until the 20th century, when most of them [migrated to Israel](351513.md).[^cite_note-Roland1998-430] The [Paradesi Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochin_Synagogue) at [Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi) is the oldest synagogue in the [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Nations).[^cite_note-431] [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism_in_Kerala) has a considerable following in the [Wayanad district](407429.md).[^cite_note-LockieCarpenter2012-432][^cite_note-MathewBaviskar2009-433]

## Education

Main article: [Education in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Kerala)



[University of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Kerala) at Thiruvananthapuram

The [Kerala school of astronomy and mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_school_of_astronomy_and_mathematics) flourished between the 14th and 16th centuries. In attempting to solve astronomical problems, the Kerala school independently created a number of important mathematics concepts, including [series expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Series_expansion) for trigonometric functions.[^cite_note-roy-434][^cite_note-435] In the early decades of the 19th century, the modern educational transformation of Kerala was triggered by the efforts of the [Church Mission Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Mission_Society) missionaries to promote mass education.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440] Following the recommendations of the [Wood's despatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wood's_despatch) of 1854, the princely states of Travancore and Cochin launched mass education drives mainly based on castes and communities, and introduced a system of [grant-in-aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grant-in-aid) to attract more private initiatives.[^cite_note-Devrep53-58-441] Catholic institutions such as [St Thomas College Thrissur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Thomas_College,_Thrissur) and [SB College Changanasserry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Berchmans_College) were established under the leadership of the Catholic Church. The efforts by leaders such as [Fr. Kuriakose Elias Chavara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuriakose_Elias_Chavara), [Mar Charles Lavigne SJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Lavigne), [Vaikunda Swami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayya_Vaikundar), [Narayana Guru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narayana_Guru) and [Ayyankali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyankali) in aiding the socially discriminated castes in the state—with the help of community-based organisations like [Nair Service Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nair_Service_Society), [SNDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sree_Narayana_Trust), [Muslim Educational Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Educational_Society), Muslim Mahajana Sabha, Yoga Kshema Sabha (of Nambudiris) and congregations of Christian churches—led to the further development of mass education in Kerala.[^cite_note-Devrep53-58-441]

In 1991, Kerala became the first state in India to be recognised as completely literate, although the effective literacy rate at that time was only 90%.[^cite_note-Soundarapandian2000-442] In 2006–2007, the state topped the Education Development Index (EDI) of the 21 major states in India.[^cite_note-443] As of 2007, enrolment in elementary education was almost 100%; and, unlike other states in India, educational opportunity was almost equally distributed among sexes, social groups, and regions.[^cite_note-Devrep255-258-444] According to the 2011 census, Kerala has a 93.9% literacy, compared to the national literacy rate of 74.0%.[^cite_note-IBNLiteracy2013-347] In January 2016, Kerala became the first Indian state to achieve 100% primary education through its *Athulyam* literacy programme.[^cite_note-445]

The educational system prevailing in the state's schools specifies an initial 10-year course of study, which is divided into three stages: lower primary, upper primary, and secondary school—known as *4+3+3*, which signifies the number of years for each stage.[^cite_note-Devrep255-258-444] After the first 10 years of schooling, students typically enroll in [Higher Secondary Schooling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_college) in one of the three major streams—[liberal arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_arts), commerce, or science.[^cite_note-Edu_Ker-446] The majority of public schools are affiliated with the [Kerala Board of Public Examination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Board_of_Public_Examination).[^cite_note-447] Other educational boards are the [Indian Certificate of Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Certificate_of_Secondary_Education) (ICSE), the [Central Board for Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Board_for_Secondary_Education) (CBSE), and the [National Institute of Open Schooling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Open_Schooling) (NIOS).[^cite_note-Edu_Ker-446]

## Culture



[Thrissur Pooram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrissur_Pooram) festival

Main article: [Culture of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Kerala)

The culture of Kerala is composite and cosmopolitan in nature and it is an integral part of [Indian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_culture).[^cite_note-Menon3-448] It is a synthesis of [Aryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Aryan_peoples), [Dravidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Indian_culture), [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_culture), and [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Europe) cultures,[^cite_note-449] developed over millennia, under influences from other parts of India and abroad.[^cite_note-Menon1978a-450] It is defined by its antiquity and the organic continuity sustained by the [Malayali people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayali_people).[^cite_note-451] It was elaborated through centuries of contact with neighbouring and overseas cultures.[^cite_note-Bhagyalekshmy_2004d_29-452] However, the geographical insularity of Kerala from the rest of the country has resulted in the development of a distinctive lifestyle, art, architecture, language, literature and social institutions.[^cite_note-Menon3-448] Over 10,000 festivals are celebrated in the state every year.[^cite_note-1000hrs-453] The [Malayalam calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_calendar), a solar sidereal calendar started from 825 CE in Kerala,[^cite_note-Devika2005-454] finds common usage in planning agricultural and religious activities.[^cite_note-Singh2004-455] [Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam), one of the classical languages in India, is Kerala's [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language).[^cite_note-Benedikter2009-456] Over a dozen other [scheduled and unscheduled languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_with_official_status_in_India) are also spoken.[^cite_note-census2011-langreport-457] Kerala has the greatest consumption of alcohol in India.[^cite_note-458]

### Festivals



During [Onam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onam), Keralites create "pookkalam" (floral carpet) designs in front of their houses.

Main articles: [Temple Festivals of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_Festivals_of_Kerala) and [Onam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onam)

Many of the temples in Kerala hold festivals on specific days of the year.[^cite_note-459] A common characteristic of these festivals is the hoisting of a holy flag which is brought down on the final day of the festival after immersing the deity.[^cite_note-460] Some festivals include Poorams, the best known of these being the [Thrissur Pooram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrissur_Pooram).[^cite_note-461] "Elephants, firework displays and huge crowds" are the major attractions of Thrissur Pooram.[^cite_note-462] Other known festivals are [Makaravilakku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makaravilakku),[^cite_note-Ltd.2013b-463] [Chinakkathoor Pooram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinakkathoor_Pooram), [Attukal Pongala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attukal_Pongala) and [Nenmara Vallangi Vela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nenmara_Vallangi_Vela)[^cite_note-Stott2014-464] Other than these, festivals locally known as [utsavams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utsavam) are conducted by many temples mostly on annual basis. Temples that can afford it will usually involve at least one richly caparisoned elephant as part of the festivities. The idol in the temple is taken out on a procession around the countryside atop this elephant. When the procession visits homes around the temple, people will usually present rice, coconuts, and other offerings to it.[^cite_note-NarayananKurup1976-465] Processions often include traditional music such as [Panchari melam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchari_melam) or [Panchavadyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchavadyam).[^cite_note-Killius2006-466] [Eid al-Fitr](154944.md) and [Eid al-Adha](358226.md) are celebrated by the Muslim community of the state while the festivals like [Christmas](6237.md) and [Easter](9325.md) are observed by the Christians.[^cite_note-Malabar-467] Onam is a harvest festival celebrated by the people of Kerala and is reminiscent of the state's agrarian past.[^cite_note-Choondal1980-468][^cite_note-Menon2007b-469] It is a local festival of Kerala[^cite_note-Darpan2006b-470] consisting of a four-day public holidays; from Onam Eve (Uthradam) to the fourth Onam Day.[^cite_note-471] Onam falls in the Malayalam month of Chingam (August–September)[^cite_note-472] and marks the commemoration of the homecoming of King [Mahabali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabali).[^cite_note-MythOnam-473] The total duration of Onam is 10 days and it is celebrated all across Kerala. It is one of the festivals celebrated with cultural elements such as [Vallam Kali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallam_Kali),[^cite_note-474] [Pulikali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulikali),[^cite_note-Ltd.2013-475] Pookkalam,[^cite_note-476] Thumbi Thullal[^cite_note-Mohapatra2013-477] and [Onavillu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onavillu).[^cite_note-(Princess.)1998-478]

### Music and dance



[Kathakali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathakali) performer



[Mohiniattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohiniattam) performance

Main articles: [Arts of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arts_of_Kerala) and [Music of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Kerala)

Kerala is home to a number of [performance arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance_art). These include five classical dance forms: [Kathakali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathakali), [Mohiniyattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohiniyattam), [Koodiyattom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koodiyattom), [Thullal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottan_thullal) and [Krishnanattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishnanattam), which originated and developed in the temple theatres during the classical period under the patronage of royal houses.[^cite_note-479] [Kerala natanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_natanam), [Thirayattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirayattam),[^cite_note-480] [Kaliyattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaliyattam), [Theyyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theyyam), [Koothu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koothu) and [Padayani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padayani) are other dance forms associated with the temple culture of the region.[^cite_note-Menon2008-481] Some traditional dance forms such as [Oppana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oppana) and [Duffmuttu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duffmuttu) were popular among the Muslims of the state,[^cite_note-google19-482] while [Margamkali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margamkali) and [Parichamuttukali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parichamuttukali) are popular among the Syrian Christians and [Chavittu nadakom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chavittu_nadakom) is popular among the Latin Christians.[^cite_note-Nārāyaṇappaṇikkar1991-483][^cite_note-Kasbekar2006-484]

The development of classical music in Kerala is attributed to the contributions it received from the traditional performance arts associated with the temple culture of Kerala.[^cite_note-Menon2-485] The development of the indigenous classical music form, [Sopana Sangeetham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sopana_Sangeetham), illustrates the rich contribution that temple culture has made to the arts of Kerala.[^cite_note-Menon2-485] [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music) dominates Keralite traditional music. This was the result of [Swathi Thirunal Rama Varma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swathi_Thirunal_Rama_Varma)'s popularisation of the genre in the 19th century.[^cite_note-Bhagyalekshmy_2004d_29-452] Raga-based renditions known as *sopanam* accompany *kathakali* performances.[^cite_note-SchechnerAppel1990-486] *Melam*, including the *paandi* and *panchari* variants, is a more percussive style of music;[^cite_note-BroughtonEllingham2000-487] it is performed at *[Kshetram](2247213.md)*-centered festivals using the *[chenda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chenda)*. *Panchavadyam* is a form of percussion ensemble, in which artists use five types of percussion instruments.[^cite_note-BroughtonEllingham2000-487] Kerala's visual arts range from [traditional murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murals_of_Kerala) to the works of [Raja Ravi Varma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Ravi_Varma), the state's most renowned painter.[^cite_note-Menon2-485] Most of the castes and communities in Kerala have rich collections of folk songs and ballads associated with a variety of themes; *[Vadakkan Pattukal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadakkan_Pattukal)* (Northern Ballads), *Thekkan pattukal* (Southern Ballads), *Vanchi pattukal* (Boat Songs), *[Mappila Pattukal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mappila_Pattukal)* (Muslim songs) and *Pallipattukal* (Church songs) are a few of them.[^cite_note-488]

### Cinema

Main article: [Malayalam cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_cinema)

[Malayalam films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_Cinema) carved a niche for themselves in the Indian film industry with the presentation of social themes.[^cite_note-Menon-489][^cite_note-490] Directors from Kerala, like [Adoor Gopalakrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoor_Gopalakrishnan), [Mankada Ravi Varma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mankada_Ravi_Varma), [G. Aravindan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._Aravindan), [Bharathan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharathan), [P. Padmarajan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._Padmarajan), [M.T. Vasudevan Nair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M.T.Vasudevan_Nair), [K.G. George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K.G._George), [Priyadarshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priyadarshan), [John Abraham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Abraham_\(director\)), [Ramu Karyat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramu_Karyat), [K S Sethumadhavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K_S_Sethumadhavan), [A. Vincent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._Vincent) and [Shaji N Karun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaji_N_Karun) have made a considerable contribution to the Indian [parallel cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_cinema). Kerala has also given birth to numerous actors, such as [Mohanlal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohanlal), [Mammootty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammootty), [Satyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyan), [Prem Nazir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prem_Nazir), [Madhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhu_\(actor\)), [Sheela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheela), [Sharada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharada_\(actress\)), [Miss Kumari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miss_Kumari), [Jayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayan), [Adoor Bhasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoor_Bhasi), [Seema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seema_\(actress\)), [Bharath Gopi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharath_Gopi), [Thilakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thilakan), [Vijaya Raghavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijaya_Raghavan), [Kalabhavan Mani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalabhavan_Mani), [Indrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indrans), [Shobana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shobana), [Nivin Pauly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nivin_Pauly), [Sreenivasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sreenivasan), [Urvashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urvashi_\(actress\)), [Manju Warrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manju_Warrier), [Suresh Gopi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suresh_Gopi), [Jayaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayaram), [Murali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murali_\(Malayalam_actor\)), [Shankaradi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shankaradi), [Kavya Madhavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavya_Madhavan), [Bhavana Menon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhavana_Menon), [Prithviraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prithviraj_Sukumaran), [Parvathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parvathy_Thiruvothu), [Jayasurya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayasurya), [Dulquer Salmaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dulquer_Salmaan), [Oduvil Unnikrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oduvil_Unnikrishnan), [Jagathy Sreekumar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagathy_Sreekumar), [Nedumudi Venu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nedumudi_Venu), [KPAC Lalitha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KPAC_Lalitha), [Innocent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innocent_\(actor\)) and [Fahadh Faasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahadh_Faasil). Late Malayalam actor Prem Nazir holds the world record for having acted as the protagonist of over 720 movies.[^cite_note-491] Since the 1980s, actors Mohanlal and Mammootty have dominated the movie industry; Mohanlal has won five National Film Awards (four for acting), while Mammootty has three National Film Awards for acting.[^cite_note-492] Malayalam Cinema has produced a few more notable personalities such as [K. J. Yesudas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._J._Yesudas), [K.S. Chitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K.S._Chitra), [M.G. Sreekumar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._G._Sreekumar), [Vayalar Rama Varma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vayalar_Rama_Varma), [V. Madhusoodanan Nair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._Madhusoodanan_Nair), [M.T. Vasudevan Nair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M.T._Vasudevan_Nair) and [O.N.V. Kurup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O.N.V._Kurup),[^cite_note-indiatimes2-493] the last two mentioned being recipients of [Jnanpith award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnanpith_award), the highest literary award in India.[^cite_note-upscguide-494] [Resul Pookutty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resul_Pookutty), who is from Kerala, is the only Indian to win an [Academy Award for Best Sound Mixing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Sound), for the breakthrough film *[Slumdog Millionaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slumdog_Millionaire)*. As of 2018, [Malayalam cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_cinema) has got 14 awards for the best actor, 6 for the best actress, 11 for the best film, and 13 for the best film director in the [National Film Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Awards), [India](14533.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018358–361-495]

### Literature

Main article: [Malayalam Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_Literature)

The [Sangam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_literature) can be considered as the ancient predecessor of [Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018450-496] [Malayalam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_literature) starts from the [Old Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Malayalam) period (9th–13th century CE) and includes such notable writers as the 14th-century [Niranam poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niranam_poets) (Madhava Panikkar, Sankara Panikkar and Rama Panikkar),[^cite_note-Nair1967-497][^cite_note-Laet1994-498] and the 16th-century poet [Thunchaththu Ezhuthachan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ezhuthachan), whose works mark the dawn of both the modern Malayalam language and its poetry.[^cite_note-George1998-499] For the first 600 years of [Malayalam calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam_Era), the literature mainly consisted of the oral [Ballads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballad) such as *[Vadakkan Pattukal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadakkan_Pattukal)* in [North Malabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Malabar) and *Thekkan Pattukal* in [Southern Travancore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Division_\(Travancore\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChandran2018453-500] Designated a "[Classical Language in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Language_in_India)" in 2013,[^cite_note-thehindu20130524-389] it developed into the current form mainly by the influence of the poets [Cherusseri Namboothiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherusseri_Namboothiri),[^cite_note-Cherussery_(Krishnagadha)_Malayalam_author_books-501][^cite_note-mlm-502] [Thunchaththu Ezhuthachan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunchaththu_Ezhuthachan),[^cite_note-mlm-502] and [Poonthanam Nambudiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poonthanam_Nambudiri),[^cite_note-mlm-502][^cite_note-503] in the 15th and the 16th centuries of [Common Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Era).[^cite_note-mlm-502][^cite_note-504] [Unnayi Variyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unnayi_Variyar),[^cite_note-Attakatha_sahithyam-505] a probable poet of the 17th/18th century CE, and [Kunchan Nambiar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunchan_Nambiar), a poet of the 18th century CE, have also influenced a lot in the growth of modern Malayalam literature in its pre-mature form.[^cite_note-mlm-502] The [Bharathappuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharathappuzha) river, also known as [River Ponnani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnani), and its tributaries, have played a major role in the development of modern Malayalam Literature.[^cite_note-506]

[Paremmakkal Thoma Kathanar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paremmakkal_Thoma_Kathanar) and [Kerala Varma Valiakoi Thampuran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Varma_Valiakoi_Thampuran) are noted for their contribution to Malayalam prose.[^cite_note-google24-507][^cite_note-Sinhā2009-508][^cite_note-Vilanilam1987-509] The "[triumvirate of poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triumvirate_poets_of_modern_malayalam)" (*Kavithrayam*): [Kumaran Asan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumaran_Asan), [Vallathol Narayana Menon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallathol_Narayana_Menon), and [Ulloor S. Parameswara Iyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulloor_S._Parameswara_Iyer), are recognised for moving Keralite poetry away from archaic sophistry and metaphysics, and towards a more [lyrical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyrics) mode.[^cite_note-Al̲ikkōṭȧ1979-510][^cite_note-Indian_and_Foreign_Review-511][^cite_note-Tarakan1990-512] The poets like [Moyinkutty Vaidyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moyinkutty_Vaidyar) and [Pulikkottil Hyder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulikkottil_Hyder) have made notable contributions to the [Mappila songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mappila_songs), which is a genre of the [Arabi Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabi_Malayalam) literature.[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] The first travelogue in any Indian language is the Malayalam *[Varthamanappusthakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varthamanappusthakam)*, written by Paremmakkal Thoma Kathanar in 1785.[^cite_note-legacy-515][^cite_note-516] The prose literature, [Malayalam journalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_journalism), and criticism began after the latter-half of the 18th century.[^cite_note-legacy-515] Contemporary Malayalam literature deals with social, political, and economic life context. The tendency of the modern literature is often towards [political radicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_radicalism).[^cite_note-517] [Malayalam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_literature) has been presented with 6 [Jnanapith awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnanpith_Award), the second-most for any Dravidian language and the third-highest for any Indian language.[^cite_note-518][^cite_note-519] In the second half of the 20th century, [Jnanpith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnanpith) winning poets and writers like [G. Sankara Kurup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._Sankara_Kurup), [S. K. Pottekkatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._K._Pottekkatt), [Thakazhi Sivasankara Pillai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thakazhi_Sivasankara_Pillai), [M. T. Vasudevan Nair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._T._Vasudevan_Nair), [O. N. V. Kurup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O._N._V._Kurup), and [Akkitham Achuthan Namboothiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkitham_Achuthan_Namboothiri), had made valuable contributions to the modern Malayalam literature.[^cite_note-google25-520][^cite_note-Accessions_List,_South_Asia-521][^cite_note-Indian_Writing_Today-522][^cite_note-DattaAkademi1987-523][^cite_note-Malayalam_Literary_Survey-524] Later, writers like [O. V. Vijayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O._V._Vijayan), [Kamaladas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamaladas), [M. Mukundan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._Mukundan), [Arundhati Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arundhati_Roy), [Vaikom Muhammed Basheer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaikom_Muhammed_Basheer), have gained international recognition.[^cite_note-MukundanPillai2004-525][^cite_note-Maheshwari2002-526][^cite_note-Chaudhuri2008-527][^cite_note-528]

### Cuisine



Onam [Sadya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadya)

Main article: [Cuisine of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Kerala)

Kerala cuisine includes a wide variety of vegetarian and non-vegetarian dishes prepared using fish, poultry, and meat. Culinary spices have been cultivated in Kerala for millennia and they are characteristic of its cuisine.[^cite_note-google26-529] Rice is a dominant staple that is eaten at all times of day.[^cite_note-Majumdar2010-530] A majority of the breakfast foods in Kerala are made out of rice, in one form or the other (*[idli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idli)*, *[dosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dosa_\(food\))*, *[puttu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puttu)*, *[pathiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathiri)*, *[appam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appam)*, or *[idiyappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiyappam)*), [tapioca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapioca) preparations, or pulse-based [vada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vada_\(food\)).[^cite_note-Muthachen1970-531] These may be accompanied by [chutney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chutney), *[kadala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chickpea)*, *[payasam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payasam)*, *[payar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mung_bean) [pappadam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pappadam)*, *appam*, chicken curry, beef fry, egg masala and fish curry.[^cite_note-Newton-268] [Porotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porotta) and [Biryani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biryani) are also often found in restaurants in Kerala. Thalassery biryani is popular as an ethnic brand. Lunch dishes include [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) and [curry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry) along with *[rasam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasam_\(dish\))*, *pulisherry* and *[sambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambar_\(dish\))*.[^cite_note-google27-532] *[Sadhya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadhya)* is a vegetarian meal, which is served on a banana leaf and followed with a cup of payasam.[^cite_note-Kannampilly2003-533] Popular snacks include [banana chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_chip), yam crisps, [tapioca chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapioca_chip), [Achappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achappam), [Unni appam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unni_appam) and *kuzhalappam*.[^cite_note-Kerala_with_Lakshadweep-534][^cite_note-Koilparampil1982-535][^cite_note-(Swami.)2000-536] Seafood specialties include [karimeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karimeen), prawns, shrimp and other crustacean dishes.[^cite_note-537] [Thalassery Cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalassery_Cuisine) is varied and is a blend of many influences.

## Media



*[Malayala Manorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayala_Manorama)* office in [Kollam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kollam)

Main article: [Media in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Kerala)

The media, telecommunications, broadcasting and cable services are regulated by the [Telecom Regulatory Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecom_Regulatory_Authority_of_India) (TRAI).[^cite_note-538] The National Family Health Survey – 4, conducted in 2015–16, ranked Kerala as the state with the [highest media exposure in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_media_exposure).[^cite_note-539] Dozens of newspapers are published in Kerala, in nine major languages,[^cite_note-rniindia-540] but principally Malayalam and English.[^cite_note-google28-541] Kerala has the highest media exposure in India.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-543] The most widely circulated [Malayalam-language newspapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Malayalam_language_newspapers) are *[Malayala Manorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayala_Manorama), [Mathrubhumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathrubhumi), [Deshabhimani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deshabhimani), [Madhyamam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhyamam), [Kerala Kaumudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Kaumudi), [Mangalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangalam_Publications), [Chandrika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandrika_\(Malayalam_newspaper\)), [Deepika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deepika_\(newspaper\)), [Janayugam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janayugam), [Janmabhumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janmabhumi), [Siraj Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siraj_Daily)* and *[Suprabhaatham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suprabhaatham_Daily)*. [Major Malayalam periodicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Malayalam-language_periodicals) include *[Mathrubhumi Azhchappathippu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathrubhumi_Azhchappathippu), [Vanitha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanitha), [India Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Today) Malayalam, [Madhyamam Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhyamam_Weekly), [Grihalakshmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grihalakshmi_\(magazine\)), [Dhanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhanam_\(business_magazine\)), [Chithrabhumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chithrabhumi)* and *[Bhashaposhini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhashaposhini)*.

[DD Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DD_Malayalam) is a state-owned television broadcaster. [Multiple-system operators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_multiple-system_operators) provide a mix of Malayalam, English, other Indian languages, and international channels. Some of the popular Malayalam television channels are [Asianet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asianet_\(TV_channel\)), [Asianet News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asianet_News), [Asianet Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asianet_Plus), [Asianet Movies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asianet_Movies), [Surya TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_TV), [Surya Movies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_Movies), [Mazhavil Manorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazhavil_Manorama), [Manorama News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manorama_News), [Kairali TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kairali_TV), Kairali News, [Flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flowers_\(TV_channel\)), [Media One TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_One_TV), [Mathrubhumi News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathrubhumi_News), [Kappa TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kappa_TV), [Amrita TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amrita_TV), [Reporter TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporter_TV), [Jaihind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaihind), [Janam TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janam_TV), [Jeevan TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeevan_TV), [Kaumudy TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaumudy_TV) and [Shalom TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalom_\(TV_channel\)). With the second-highest internet penetration rate in India,[^cite_note-544] [Digital media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_media) including [social media](5897742.md) and [OTT services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over-the-top_media_services_in_India) are a main source of information and entertainment in the state.
