A sizeable [people's science movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_science_movement) has taken root in the state, and activities like writer's cooperatives are becoming increasingly common.[^cite_note-Tharamangalam_2005-228][^cite_note-Ranjith_2004-545] [BSNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSNL), [Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [Vodafone Idea Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea_Limited), [Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jio) are the major cell phone service providers.[^cite_note-546] Broadband Internet services are widely available throughout the state; some of the major [Internet service providers](100245.md) are BSNL, [Asianet Satellite Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asianet_Satellite_Communications), Airtel, Vodafone Idea Limited, [RailWire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RailTel), and [Tata Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Communications).

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Kerala)



The annual [snake boat race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallam_Kali) is performed during [Onam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onam) on the [Pamba River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamba_River)

By the 21st century, almost all of the native sports and games from Kerala had either disappeared or become just an art form performed during local festivals; including [Poorakkali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poorakkali), Padayani, Thalappandukali, Onathallu, Parichamuttukali, Velakali, and Kilithattukali.[^cite_note-PRD-sports-547] However, *[Kalaripayattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalaripayattu)*, regarded as "the mother of all martial arts in the world", is an exception and is practised as the indigenous martial sport.[^cite_note-548] Another traditional sport of Kerala is the boat race, especially the race of [snake boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snake_boat).[^cite_note-PRD-sports-547]

[Cricket](25675557.md) and [football](10568.md) became popular in the state; both were introduced in Malabar during the British colonial period in the 19th century. Cricketers, like [Tinu Yohannan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tinu_Yohannan), [Abey Kuruvilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abey_Kuruvilla), [Chundangapoyil Rizwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chundangapoyil_Rizwan), [Sreesanth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sreesanth), [Sanju Samson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanju_Samson) and [Basil Thampi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basil_Thampi) found places in the national cricket team. A cricket franchise from Kerala, the [Kochi Tuskers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi_Tuskers_Kerala), played in the [Indian Premier League](13260340.md)'s [fourth season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Indian_Premier_League). However, this team was disbanded after the season because of conflicts of interest among its franchises.[^cite_note-India_Wins_World_Twenty20_Thriller-549][^cite_note-hindu_jul09-550] Kerala has only performed well recently in the [Ranji Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranji_Trophy) cricket competition, in 2017–18 reaching the quarterfinals for the first time in history.[^cite_note-PRD-sports-547][^cite_note-551] Football is one of the most widely played and watched sports with huge in this state support for club and district level matches. Kochi hosts [Kerala Blasters FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Blasters_FC) in the [Indian Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Super_League). The Blasters are one of the most widely supported clubs in the country as well as the fifth most-followed football club from [Asia](689.md) in social media.[^cite_note-552][^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554] Also, Kozhikode hosts [Gokulam Kerala FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gokulam_Kerala_FC) in the [I-League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-League) as well as the [Sait Nagjee Football Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sait_Nagjee_Football_Tournament). Kerala is one of the major footballing states in India along with West Bengal and Goa and has produced national players like [I. M. Vijayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I._M._Vijayan), C. V. Pappachan, [V. P. Sathyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._P._Sathyan), [U. Sharaf Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U._Sharaf_Ali), [Jo Paul Ancheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo_Paul_Ancheri), [Ashique Kuruniyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashique_Kuruniyan), [Muhammad Rafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Rafi_\(footballer\)), [Jiju Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiju_Jacob), [Mashoor Shereef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mashoor_Shereef), [Pappachen Pradeep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pappachen_Pradeep), [C.K. Vineeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C.K._Vineeth), [Anas Edathodika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anas_Edathodika), [Sahal Abdul Samad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahal_Abdul_Samad), and [Rino Anto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rino_Anto).[^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556][^cite_note-india-557][^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559] The Kerala state football team has won the [Santhosh Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santhosh_Trophy) seven times; in 1973, 1992, 1993, 2001, 2004, 2018, and 2022. They were also the runners-up eight times.[^cite_note-560]

Among the prominent athletes hailing from the state are [P. T. Usha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._T._Usha), [Shiny Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiny_Wilson) and [M.D. Valsamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M.D._Valsamma), all three of whom are recipients of the [Padma Shri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padma_Shri) as well as [Arjuna Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arjuna_Award), while [K. M. Beenamol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._M._Beenamol) and [Anju Bobby George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anju_Bobby_George) are [Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_Khel_Ratna) and Arjuna Award winners. [T. C. Yohannan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T._C._Yohannan), [Suresh Babu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suresh_Babu_\(long_jumper\)), [Sinimol Paulose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinimol_Paulose), Angel Mary Joseph, [Mercy Kuttan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Kuttan), K. Saramma, [K. C. Rosakutty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa_Kutty), Padmini Selvan and Tintu Luka are the other Arjuna Award winners from Kerala.[^cite_note-PRD-sports-547][^cite_note-561] Volleyball is another popular sport and is often played on makeshift courts on sandy beaches along the coast.[^cite_note-562] [Jimmy George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_George) was a notable Indian volleyball player, rated in his prime as among the world's ten best players.[^cite_note-563] Other popular sports include [badminton](3956.md), [basketball](3921.md) and [kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi).[^cite_note-Reddy2005-564] The Indian Hockey team captain [P. R. Shreejesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._R._Shreejesh), ace goalkeeper hails from Kerala. International Walkers from the state include [K. T. Irfan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._T._Irfan).[^cite_note-565]

For the [2017 FIFA U-17 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_U-17_World_Cup) in [India](14533.md), the [Jawaharlal Nehru Stadium (Kochi)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Stadium_\(Kochi\)), was chosen as one of the six venues where the game would be hosted in India.[^cite_note-566] [Greenfield International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenfield_International_Stadium) at located at Kariavattom in Thiruvananthapuram city, is India's first DBOT (design, build, operate and transfer) model outdoor stadium and it has hosted international cricket matches and international football matches including [2015 SAFF Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_SAFF_Championship).[^cite_note-SAFF_Cup_Dates-567]

## Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Kerala)

Kerala's culture and traditions, coupled with its varied [demographics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Kerala), have made the state one of the most popular tourist destinations in India. In 2012, [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Society)'s *[Traveller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Traveler)* magazine named Kerala as one of the "ten paradises of the world"[^cite_note-Kerala_Tourism-568][^cite_note-569][^cite_note-570] and "50 must see destinations of a lifetime".[^cite_note-Kerala_India-571] *[Travel and Leisure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_and_Leisure)* also described Kerala as "One of the 100 great trips for the 21st century".[^cite_note-Kerala_Tourism-568][^cite_note-572] In 2012, it overtook the [Taj Mahal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taj_Mahal) to be the number one travel destination in Google's search trends for India.[^cite_note-573] [CNN Travel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN_Travel) listed Kerala among its '19 best places to visit in 2019'.[^cite_note-574] Kerala was named by [*TIME* magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\)) in 2022 among the 50 extraordinary destinations to explore in its list of the World's Greatest Places.[^cite_note-575] In 2025, Kerala was ranked 16th in the [Rough Guides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rough_Guides)' list of the "World's Top 26 Destinations for 2026", making it the only Indian state to be included in the international travel guide's annual global destinations ranking.[^cite_note-576]

Kerala's beaches, backwaters, lakes, mountain ranges, waterfalls, ancient ports, palaces, religious institutions[^cite_note-Ltd.2012-577] and wildlife sanctuaries are major attractions for both domestic and international tourists.[^cite_note-Tripays-578] The city of Kochi ranks first in the total number of international and domestic tourists in Kerala.[^cite_note-keralatourism-579][^cite_note-Tourism_Statistics-580] Until the early 1980s, Kerala was a relatively unknown destination compared to other states in the country.[^cite_note-HinduTourism-581] In 1986 the government of Kerala declared tourism an important industry and it was the first state in India to do so.[^cite_note-Kerala_Tourism-568] Marketing campaigns launched by the [Kerala Tourism Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Tourism_Development_Corporation), the government agency that oversees the tourism prospects of the state, resulted in the growth of the tourism industry.[^cite_note-Tourism-582] Many advertisements branded Kerala with the tagline *[Kerala, God's Own Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God's_Own_Country)*.[^cite_note-Tourism-582] Kerala tourism is a global brand and regarded as one of the destinations with highest recall.[^cite_note-Tourism-582] In 2006, Kerala attracted 8.5 million tourists, an increase of 23.7% over the previous year, making the state one of the fastest-growing popular destinations in the world.[^cite_note-583] In 2011, tourist inflow to Kerala crossed the 10-million mark.[^cite_note-bizstd-584]

Ayurvedic tourism has become very popular since the 1990s, and private agencies have played a notable role in tandem with the initiatives of the Tourism Department.[^cite_note-HinduTourism-581] Kerala is known for its [ecotourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecotourism) initiatives which include mountaineering, trekking and bird-watching programmes in the Western Ghats as the major activities.[^cite_note-Devrep47-585] The state's tourism industry is a major contributor to the [state's economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Kerala), growing at the rate of 13.3%.[^cite_note-586] The revenue from tourism increased five-fold between 2001 and 2011 and crossed the ₹ 190 billion mark in 2011. According to the Economic Times[^cite_note-587] Kerala netted a record revenue of INR 365280.1 million from the tourism sector in 2018, clocking an increase of Rs 28743.3 million from the previous year. Over 16.7 million tourists visited Kerala in 2018 as against 15.76 million the previous year, recording an increase of 5.9%. The industry provides employment to approximately 1.2 million people.[^cite_note-bizstd-584]

The state's only drive-in beach, [Muzhappilangad Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzhappilangad_Beach) in Kannur, which stretches across 5 kilometres (3.1 mi) of sand, was chosen by the BBC as one of the top six drive-in beaches in the world in 2016.[^cite_note-588] [Idukki Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idukki_Dam), the world's second arch dam, and Asia's first is at [Idukki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idukki). The major beaches are at [Kovalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kovalam), [Varkala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varkala), [Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode), [Fort Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Kochi), [Cherai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherai), [Alappuzha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alappuzha), [Ponnani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnani), [Kadalundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadalundi), [Tanur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanur,_Malappuram), [Chaliyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaliyam), [Payyambalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payyambalam), Kappad, Muzhappilangad and [Bekal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bekal). Popular [hill stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_station) are at [Ponmudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponmudi), [Wayanad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayanad), [Vagamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vagamon), [Munnar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munnar), [Peermade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peermade), [Ramakkalmedu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramakkalmedu), [Arimbra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arimbra_Hills,_Malappuram), [Paithalmala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paithalmala) of Kannur district, [Kodikuthimala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodikuthimala), and [Nelliampathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelliampathi).[^cite_note-589] Munnar is 4,500 feet above sea level and is known for tea plantations, and a variety of flora and fauna.[^cite_note-FPJ-590] Kerala's ecotourism destinations include 12 wildlife sanctuaries and two national parks: [Periyar Tiger Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periyar_Tiger_Reserve), [Parambikulam Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parambikulam_Wildlife_Sanctuary), [Chinnar Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinnar_Wildlife_Sanctuary), [Thattekad Bird Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thattekad_Bird_Sanctuary), [Wayanad Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayanad_Wildlife_Sanctuary), [Kadalundi Bird Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadalundi_Bird_Sanctuary), [Karimpuzha Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karimpuzha_Wildlife_Sanctuary), [Muthanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muthanga) Wildlife Sanctuary, [Aralam Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aralam_Wildlife_Sanctuary), [Eravikulam National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eravikulam_National_Park), and [Silent Valley National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_Valley_National_Park) are the most popular among them.[^cite_note-591] The Kerala backwaters are an extensive network of interlocking rivers (41 west-flowing rivers), lakes, and canals that centre around Alleppey, [Kumarakom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumarakom), Ponnani, [Nileshwaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nileshwaram), and [Punnamada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punnamada) (where the annual Nehru Trophy Boat Race is held in August), [Pathiramanal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathiramanal) a small island in [Muhamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhamma). [Padmanabhapuram Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padmanabhapuram_Palace) and the [Mattancherry Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattancherry_Palace) are two nearby heritage sites.[^cite_note-592][^cite_note-593]

[^cite_note-roy-434]: &#x20;Roy, Ranjan (1990). "Discovery of the Series Formula for π by Leibniz, Gregory, and Nilakantha". *Mathematics Magazine*. **63** (5): 291–306. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.2307/2690896](https://doi.org/10.2307%2F2690896). [JSTOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JSTOR_\(identifier\)) [2690896](https://www.jstor.org/stable/2690896).

[^cite_note-435]: &#x20;[Pingree, David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Pingree) (1992), "Hellenophilia versus the History of Science", *Isis*, **83** (4): 554–63, [Bibcode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibcode_\(identifier\)):[1992Isis...83..554P](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/1992Isis...83..554P), [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1086/356288](https://doi.org/10.1086%2F356288), [JSTOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JSTOR_\(identifier\)) [234257](https://www.jstor.org/stable/234257), [S2CID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S2CID_\(identifier\)) [68570164](https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID\:68570164), One example I can give you relates to the Indian Mādhava's demonstration, in about 1400 A.D., of the infinite power series of trigonometrical functions using geometrical and algebraic arguments. When this was first described in English by Charles Whish, in the 1830s, it was heralded as the Indians' discovery of the calculus. This claim and Mādhava's achievements were ignored by Western historians, presumably at first because they could not admit that an Indian discovered the calculus, but later because no one read anymore the *Transactions of the Royal Asiatic Society*, in which Whish's article was published. The matter resurfaced in the 1950s, and now we have the Sanskrit texts properly edited, and we understand the clever way that Mādhava derived the series *without* the calculus, but many historians still find it impossible to conceive of the problem and its solution in terms of anything other than the calculus and proclaim that the calculus is what Mādhava found. In this case, the elegance and brilliance of Mādhava's mathematics are being distorted as they are buried under the current mathematical solution to a problem to which he discovered an alternate and powerful solution.

[^cite_note-436]: &#x20;["Missionaries led State to renaissance: Pinarayi"](https://www.thehindu.com/news/national/kerala/Missionaries-led-State-to-renaissance-Pinarayi/article16444369.ece). *The Hindu*. 13 November 2016. Inaugurating on Saturday the valedictory of the bicentenary celebration of the arrival of Church Mission Society (CMS) missionaries to the shores of Kerala, Mr. Vijayan said it was their pioneering work in the fields of education, literature, printing, publishing, women's education, education of the differently-abled and, in general, a new social approach through the inclusion of marginalised sections into the mainstream which brought the idea of 'equality' into the realm of public consciousness. This had raised the standard of public consciousness and paved the way for the emergence of the renaissance movements in the State.

[^cite_note-437]: &#x20;["Kerala to celebrate CMS mission"](https://churchmissionsociety.org/our-stories/kerala-celebrate-cms-mission). *Church Mission Society*. 9 November 2016. Retrieved 4 April 2022. Indian President Pranab Mukherjee, visited CMS College in Kerala, the oldest college in India, and laid the foundation stone of the bicentenary block. He said, 'CMS college is a pioneer of modern education in Kerala. It has been the source of strong currents of knowledge and critical inquiry that have moulded the scholastic and socio-cultural landscape of Kerala and propelled the State to the forefront of social development.'&#x20;
